### India Targets Journalists in Crackdown on Growing Protest Movement
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-19 21:20 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gen-z-india-protests-journalists-press-freedom-new-delhi-cockroach-janata-party-demonstrations-youth-journalists-detained-jailed/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

As protests continue across India over allegations of government corruption, dozens of members of the media have been subjected to arrest, detainment and assault, according to witnesses, press rights organizations, and the journalists themselves. Thousands of protesters have taken to the streets of New Delhi in recent weeks, demanding the resignation of election officials allegedly tied to the deletion of nearly 130 million names from voter registration rolls. The protests — which have largely been organized by the Cockroach Janata Party, so named after India's chief justice called the nation’s unemployed youth and activists "cockroaches" — are part of a wider wave of youth discontent aimed at Prime Minister Narendra Modi and his administration. Organizers have mobilized young people via social media, tapping into frustrations with government corruption, eroding trust in democratic institutions, and a lack of access to jobs and educational opportunities.

### Testing the Legal Limits of Press Access
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-15 14:51 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/press-access-trump-white-house-press-pool-political-cnn-reporting-president-justice-department-judge/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The Trump administration’s latest attacks on the news media have followed a familiar pattern: President Donald Trump lashes out at news outlets for perceived bias and follows up with restrictive actions that appear blatantly unconstitutional; a judge’s ruling follows, temporarily halting the actions; and the administration pivots and pushes ahead with other restrictions. This time it was the removal of three outlets — CNN, MS NOW, and Politico — from White House press access in September. After the groups sued, and U.S. District Judge Timothy Kelly issued a temporary restraining order restoring their access on Sept. 24, the administration barred CNN and Politico from providing pool coverage on trips outside Washington. Kelly issued a longer, preliminary injunction on Oct. 14, halting the administration from blocking the news organizations’ access to the White House while the case proceeds. Meanwhile, in another escalation involving TV pool coverage of the White House, the Justice Department announced Oct.

### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/independent-journalism-new-newsroom-launch-otter-news-daniel-drepper-business-plan-climate-crisis-environmental-journalism/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

Source: The Guardian

Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

John Healey is expected to cut red tape requirements, particularly for smaller businesses Here are some more lines from Patrick Maguire ’s interview with John Healey in the Times . This passage sums up the overall picture very well. Maguire writes: So what is the chancellor now concentrating on? One could be forgiven for thinking him utterly unreconstructed: a party man and proud of it. There is no such thing as Healeyism, he says when the conversation turns to Anthony Crosland’s evangelism for the mixed economy: “I’m just Labour.” “Just Labour” is exactly what the bond markets fear — and, indeed, it is what gives Kemi Badenoch hope of pulling off a comeback to rank alongside Lazarus and Bobby Ewing for utter implausibility. Healey argued that his budget next week would refute the claim made by Andy Haldane, the former Bank of England chief economist, about Labour not having the right policies to promote growth .

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

### Mali: Army, Russia’s ‘Africa Corps’ Commit New Abuses
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/22/mali-army-russias-africa-corps-commit-new-abuses
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Aftermath of the apparent Malian military drone strike on September 16, 2026, in Guélédié village, Mopti region, Mali. Screenshots from video analyzed and geolocated by Human Rights Watch. © Private (Nairobi) – Mali’s armed forces and the allied Russia-controlled Africa Corps have committed new atrocities against civilians in central Mali during renewed fighting, Human Rights Watch said today. On September 16, 2026, an apparent military drone strike in Guélédié village, Mopti region, killed eight civilians and injured at least six others. Between May and August, Malian and Africa Corps forces held numerous detainees inhumanely in a metal container at an army base in Sévaré town, Mopti region, where many were tortured, including with severe beatings, electric shock, and waterboarding. “Civilians are bearing the brunt of the Mali junta’s failure to rein in its abusive forces and allies,” said Ilaria Allegrozzi, senior Sahel researcher at Human Rights Watch.

### Burkina Faso Restores Death Penalty
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-21 17:17 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/21/burkina-faso-restores-death-penalty
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Burkina Faso soldiers patrol aboard a pickup truck on the road from Dori to the Goudebo refugee camp, on February 3, 2020. © 2020 OLYMPIA DE MAISMONT/AFP via Getty Images Burkina Faso’s unelected parliament has unanimously adopted a new penal code that restores the death penalty for high treason, terrorism, acts of espionage, parricide and importation of expired or prohibited products. The country had abolished the death penalty for ordinary crimes in 2018 while its last known judicial execution took place in 1988. Reinstating capital punishment sends a chilling message to critics of the military junta and risks becoming another tool to suppress dissent. Since the September 2022 military coup, Burkina Faso’s junta has steadily dismantled civic and political rights. Authorities have banned media outlets, suspended unions and civil society organizations, abolished multiparty politics, and subjected critics to arbitrary detention, enforced disappearances, unlawful conscription, and torture.

Click to expand Image A worker walks past a sign with the Vietnamese Communist Party flag in Hanoi, October 5, 2020. © 2020 NHAC NGUYEN/AFP via Getty Images The Vietnamese government is systematically suppressing the rights of workers to organize independent unions despite trade agreements that require it to support labor rights.Officials have repeatedly made false, misleading, and deceptive claims about supporting independent unions while making clear that they would target anyone who tried to exercise labor rights. The government routinely used criminal laws to prosecute rights activists.Vietnam’s government needs to carry out reforms it pledged to implement. Its trading partners need to face reality and make clear to the government that its broken pledges on labor rights will carry consequences. (New York) ‒ The Vietnamese government is systematically suppressing the rights of workers to organize independent unions, Human Rights Watch said in a report released today.

### US: Order ‘No ICE’ Zones during Election Period
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-20 10:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/20/us-order-no-ice-zones-during-election-period
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A voter carries a ballot during early voting in the New York City mayoral elections on October 25, 2025. © 2025 Olga Fedorova/AP Photo (Washington, DC) – The United States government should order federal Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) officers not to operate in the vicinity of polling places, early voting locations, or drop boxes during the midterm electoral period, Human Rights Watch said today. The presence of these federal agents could deter people from voting. “ICE officers’ abuses have already seriously affected US citizens who have been stopped, beaten, detained, deported, and even killed,” said Philippe Bolopion, executive director at Human Rights Watch. “Voters should not have to fear being caught in an ICE dragnet when they head to the polls.” In-person early voting is already taking place in the majority of states.

### Guatemala: Prominent Anti-Corruption Lawyer Fatally Shot
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-20 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/20/guatemala-prominent-anti-corruption-lawyer-fatally-shot
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image People stand near a framed photo of Guatemalan lawyer and anti-corruption figure Juan Francisco Solorzano Foppa, who died after being shot by unknown assailants on Saturday while visiting a sports center, during his wake at a funeral home in Guatemala City, Guatemala, October 19, 2026. © 2026 Cristina Chiquin/Reuters (Washington, DC) – Guatemalan authorities should carry out a thorough, prompt, and credible investigation into the killing of Juan Francisco Solórzano Foppa, a prominent anti-corruption lawyer, former prosecutor, and former head of Guatemala’s tax agency, Human Rights Watch said today. On October 17, 2026, armed assailants shot and killed Solórzano Foppa at the Campo Marte sports complex in Guatemala City. Another man, identified by authorities as Gustavo Adolfo Aguilar Gamarro, was also killed in the attack. Guatemalan authorities arrested two men in connection with the crime, but prosecutors said there was no evidence linking them to the attack and a judge ordered their release.

Published: 2026-10-19 18:15 UTC

Click to expand Image Riyadh's general court, Saudi Arabia, October 20, 2008. © 2008 Fahad Shadeed/Reuters (Beirut) – Saudi authorities announced on October 17, 2026, that they had executed Hassan Zaki al-Faraj, who was convicted of crimes allegedly committed when he was a 14-year-old boy, Human Rights Watch said today. The Supreme Court has also apparently upheld the death sentence of another man, Yousef al-Manasif, who was convicted of crimes allegedly committed when he was 15 and 16, putting him at risk of imminent execution. At least three other men convicted of alleged crimes committed as children are on death row, despite allegations of torture, forced confessions, and prolonged solitary confinement. “The execution of Hassan al-Faraj is unconscionable compounded by alleged torture-tainted evidence and a well-documented record of unfair trials,” said Joey Shea, senior Saudi Arabia researcher at Human Rights Watch. “With the Supreme Court’s recent decision, Yousef al-Manasif is also at serious risk of execution.

### The Why and How of Foreign Aid -- by Dean Karlan
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35926#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Foreign aid has become a melting pot of motivations and policies, where differently-motivated actors often converge on recommended policies while the similarly-motivated diverge. Breaking down the “why” and the “how” can illuminate real tensions and reveal false ones. The “why” asks which problems to tackle and who benefits: the recipient, the donor through economic, political, or soft-power returns, or everyone through global public goods. The “how” asks which strategy most cost-effectively addresses a chosen problem. Evidence is central to the “how”: use it where it exists, produce it where it does not. In particular, approaches which improve state capacity to use and generate evidence can satisfy multiple objectives: strengthen recipient countries, benefit donors, and provide a global public good of knowledge. Ultimately, the “how” is not a linear process, but rather an iterative one of learning, adopting, adapting, repeating, and then sharing so others can learn as well.

### Correcting for Heterogeneity in the Use of Subjective Scales -- by Daniel J. Benjamin, Kristen B. Cooper, Ori Heffetz, Miles S. Kimball, Jiannan Zhou
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35925#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Analyses of survey data may be confounded if people use response scales differently. We formalize and generalize the theory behind using “calibration questions,” such as vignettes, to correct for scale-use heterogeneity. Finding, in our (N ≈ 10,000) and existing data, that the mapping between respondents’ scales is approximately linear, we develop a simple estimator for scale-use-corrected regressions. We validate it using subjective-scale ratings of height and weight and illustrate it with life satisfaction regressions, where correction leaves the main qualitative findings intact but can substantially change coefficient ratios, for example tripling the estimated cost of unemployment relative to income.

### Too Little too Late? Nationwide Carbon Trading in China -- by Shuang Zhang, Douglas Almond
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35924#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

China launched a nationwide, rate-based carbon market in 2021. Covering the world’s top CO2 emitter, compliance depends on the intensity of carbon emissions rather than an absolute cap. We draw on the theoretical literature of rate-based systems to provide the first empirical evidence on an early adopter. Using plant-level data from satellite imagery and machine learning methods, we find that high-efficiency power plants increased electricity generation and CO2 emissions by approximately 5% relative to low-efficiency plants. Because its carbon market regulates nearly all coal plants in China, we focus on the differential responses by plant efficiency, finding no evidence of a differential reduction in emission intensity (though these estimates are imprecise). Relative to coal plants in Indonesia and the Philippines, which had no carbon market, China’s high-efficiency plants also increased emissions, while the estimate for its low-efficiency plants is small and insignificant.

### Staying Connected: Experimental Effects of Coach-Delivered Text Messages in an Adult High School -- by Alberto Ortega, Pietro Pellerito, Patrick S. Turner
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35923#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

More than 23 million adults in the United States lack a high school diploma, and most who return to school drop out before graduating. We evaluate a randomized intervention of adult high school students in which coaches delivered text messages that encouraged persistence. We find that assignment to treatment increased graduation by 4.9 percentage points, and the effect of message receipt among compliers is 9.3 percentage points, a 41 percent increase relative to the control mean. The intervention operated on the intensive margin by increasing the number of courses completed, but did not affect student enrollment. The effects of the intervention are concentrated among students assigned to experienced coaches, who customized their messages more extensively. We contextualize the economic impact of the low-cost intervention by comparing labor market earnings of graduates to non-graduates, which implies an earnings benefit of $149 per treated student over four years.

### Will Medical Care Strangle or Save the Federal Budget? -- by David M. Cutler
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35922#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Forecasts of the federal budget depend greatly on trends in medical spending. Since 1970, medical spending has nearly tripled as a share of gross domestic product (GDP). However, spending growth has barely exceeded GDP growth since 2010. This difference in growth rates makes forecasting spending difficult. I present scenarios for future spending derived from considering the sources of spending increase, ranging from rapid increases in new, expensive treatments to a reduction in spending due to cost-reducing technology. Even modest increases in spending relative to GDP are likely to overwhelm the federal government’s ability to pay for medical care using current tax instruments.

### Understanding Migration Responses to Local Shocks -- by Kirill Borusyak, Rafael Dix-Carneiro, Brian K. Kovak
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35921#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A commonly used migration regression relates population changes to local labor demand shocks, often yielding small or statistically insignificant coefficients despite sizable effects on labor market outcomes. We argue that such findings need not imply that mobility is unresponsive to local economic conditions. Rather, conventional migration regressions can be biased, as they do not reflect how workers choose among competing locations. In a parsimonious equilibrium model, local population growth depends on differences between local shocks and those in relevant destinations; when these shocks are correlated, estimated responses are attenuated. Moreover, with industry-level shocks, standard shift-share measures misrepresent locations’ effective exposure, leading to further attenuation. Using simulation experiments based on Brazilian and German administrative data, we show attenuation can exceed 50% and that conventional regression estimates can be highly imprecise.

### Mind the gap: Bringing EU debt into the Stability and Growth Pact
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/mind-gap-bringing-eu-debt-stability-and-growth-pact
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

EU debt has become a noticeable feature of Europe’s fiscal architecture. However, EU fiscal rules focus solely on national public finances. They have a blind spot for EU debt, which calls their goal of fiscal sustainability into question. This column proposes a simple way to bridge this gap. Under our proposal, additional EU borrowing would be matched by a corresponding reduction in national borrowing. The mechanism would make the fiscal burden of EU debt visible in national statistics without reopening the recently reformed rules. It could also strengthen the safe-asset properties of EU bonds.

### Establishment-level evidence on California’s fast-food minimum wage
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/establishment-level-evidence-californias-fast-food-minimum-wage
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The standard prediction is that a higher minimum wage reduces employment among low-wage workers. This column examines the effect of a rise in the minimum wage in California for workers at both large and small fast-food chains. Among smaller chains, employment, the number of establishments, and openings fell, while closures rose. The largest chains remained unchanged, indicating that the policy shifted California’s fast-food market towards its largest chains.

### Investment screening reshapes rather than shrinks foreign investment
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/investment-screening-reshapes-rather-shrinks-foreign-investment
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Driven by national security concerns, a growing number of countries screen foreign investment in an expanding range of sectors, yet evidence on the economic effects of such screening is scarce. This column uses new sector-level data for 43 OECD and EU countries to show that screening reduces the number of cross-border acquisitions by 11–18% but leaves the total value of inward investment unchanged. There is evidence for re-structuring of deals: minority stakes fall while controlling acquisitions rise. The effects are not concentrated on investors from China or other politically non-aligned countries.

### Lower capital requirements are no shortcut to European competitiveness
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/lower-capital-requirements-are-no-shortcut-european-competitiveness
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The European banking sector is known for high regulatory complexity, market fragmentation, and uneven implementation of international standards. However, this column argues that across-the-board reductions in capital requirements are not the solution. European requirements are not uniformly higher than US requirements, and capital rules do not mainly explain the past gap in return on equity, which is now largely closed. Lower capital requirements are also unlikely to unlock additional lending for early-stage technologies and innovative firms. Instead, reforms should focus on simplifying the interaction between different requirements, streamlining reporting, and completing the Banking Union.

### Assainissement budgétaire : coûts temporaires ou séquelles durables
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-20 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/assainissement-budgetaire-couts-temporaires-ou-sequelles-durables
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

La dette publique atteint ou frôle des niveaux records dans l’ensemble des pays de l’OCDE, ce qui accentue la pression exercée sur les gouvernements pour qu’ils rétablissent la viabilité budgétaire à terme. Cet article s’appuie sur de nouvelles données comparatives entre pays qui montrent que les coûts en termes de production liés à l’assainissement budgétaire sont, en moyenne, modestes et de courte durée, et qu’il est difficile de mettre en évidence des séquelles durables. Mais les moyennes masquent une asymétrie cruciale : les assainissements lancés dans un contexte de forte endettement sont nettement plus restrictifs, et dans plus de la moitié des cas de l’échantillon, le ratio d’endettement était encore plus élevé trois ans après l’assainissement. La situation budgétaire initiale a davantage d’incidence sur les résultats que le choix entre réductions des dépenses et hausses d’impôts.

### What happens to innovation after Big Tech buys a startup
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-19 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/what-happens-innovation-after-big-tech-buys-startup
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Concerns abound that ‘killer acquisitions’ by tech giants could deter startup entry and stifle innovation. This column analyses patent data before and after 1,200 acquisitions by eight technology giants between 1995 and 2020. On average, the ‘Big Eight’ firms appear to invest in the acquired technology rather than kill it. Acquired inventions become more influential, more widely cited, and more embedded in follow-on innovation by both the acquirer and competitors. However, longer-term changes to the competitive landscape need further investigation.

### The sci-fi inspired “cloaking device” makes invisibility possible
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-22 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/invisibility-cloak-183582/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

For as long as human beings have been writing about fantasy, myth, and science fiction, the dream of invisibility has always been a top priority. There would be so much you could do or accomplish, under a stealthy guise, if only you didn’t have to worry about being seen. While Star Trek brought the idea of a cloaking device into the popular consciousness, and Harry Potter brought with it the widespread idea of an actual invisibility cloak, there haven’t been many useful applications of invisibility-based technology on a large scale. In fact, the closest we’ve come to achieving actual invisibility has been through the development of stealth technology, which only bestows a rudimentary invisibility at far longer wavelengths than human eyes can perceive by making the your target effectively smaller than the device itself.

### Exploring Unsupervised Methods for Recovering the Refusal Direction in LLMs
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-22 02:05 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/oTnoQbqnXiNB5umkD/exploring-unsupervised-methods-for-recovering-the-refusal
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Introduction This is the first part of what we plan to be a research exploration into the geometries of LLMs. We start with building off existing work by Arditi et al focused on the refusal direction in LLMs. Background We recommend reading the Arditi et al. Lesswrong post and paper for more details. They explore the idea of the behavior categorized as Refusal being mediated by a single direction. Refusal is basically when an LLM refuses to do something, so examples like "I cannot fulfill..." "I cannot complete..." etc. A direction is defined by a vector. Here, it means a vector in an LLM's activation space. An LLM's activation space is the space where an LLM's internal vectors live. Think of the intermediary vectors that are used in the calculation of the final next-token-prediction process. Mediated means that if this direction were to be removed, then the behavior categorized as Refusal would stop. Arditi et al. performs directional ablation as their method of removal. They identify the refusal direction using difference-in-means .

### Olmo's Kitchen: Probing a Model's Behaviour Across Various Checkpoints.
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 23:26 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/rtGq2KyurpauvkboL/olmo-s-kitchen-probing-a-model-s-behaviour-across-various
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Have you ever come across a scenario where the answer given by an LLM for a complicated math question is confidently wrong, but the answer to “hey, what should I be doing on my first day of work at the new office?” sounds like exactly what you need to hear? Or vice versa? Can we say that the training set domain or training stage domain influences the model’s traits and behaviour for off-domain knowledge and instructions? I began testing this with the open-source checkpoints of the Olmo 3 7B Think model. This model has three training stages in its pipeline, namely supervised fine-tuning (SFT), direct preference optimization(DPO), and reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards(RLVR), available publicly. Now hold up. What does all that even mean? Consider the example of training an amateur chef. The chef is first made to work under the guidance of the Gordon Ramsey of the restaurant. They are given a rulebook and asked to follow, copy, and learn everything that the expert in the field does. The chef becomes a helpful assistant.

### What's in CODI's latent recurrent thoughts?
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 22:43 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/yetmqjCXEav9iaJGw/what-s-in-codi-s-latent-recurrent-thoughts
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

What's in CODI's latent recurrent thoughts? TL;DR CODI is a model using latent reasoning. Previous work by Cywiński et al. (2025) has found that CODI's latent recurrent states (“thoughts”) do causally carry a computation's intermediate values, in a way that can be indirectly detected with LogitLens, though the thoughts themselves remained opaque. We here ask whether it is possible use activation verbalizers (AVs: NLAs and AOs) to still decode them. To this end, we train a Qwen3-8B CODI to connect to current AVs. We find that LogitLens and J-lens read values only from late layers, but AVs recover the final answer and intermediate values weakly but significantly, including from the thoughts themselves. This suggests that the values are in fact encoded, but requires more expressivity to decode. However, we find that editing the surfaced values rarely steers the model output towards the corresponding counterfactual answer.

### D̶o̶n̶’̶t̵⠀Can’t Look Up
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-21 22:11 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/2YmtD4BLTqaBketsp/d-o-n-t-can-t-look-up
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Why AI development won’t stop and what can realistically be done about it. If war is so terrible, why do we still actively wage it? If nuclear weapons are so powerful and dangerous that we even came up with the concept of Mutually Assured Destruction (MAD) as a consequence of using them, why do we still keep stockpiles of them? If climate change is a scientifically proven fact whose consequences are not only awful, but global-reaching, why haven’t we successfully addressed it yet? If hunger could have already been eradicated, why haven’t we done so? If many of the most renowned AI scientists have warned against AI’s reckless development , why don’t we pause its development until at least we feel more certain about how to do it safely? These questions are complex and difficult to address in the world we live in. Or in other words: if these problems were easy to manage, they would already have been solved.

Like much modern fantasy, Philip Pullman’s His Dark Materials series features magical objects. Each book in the trilogy is named after one; the second is The Subtle Knife . This knife looks unremarkable, but when wielded by the right person, it can cut a hole in the world — a portal into an alternate universe. Unfortunately, the portal’s edges also turn out to be a wound in the fabric of reality … and the wound bleeds. Sinister, invisible things leak out, hungry ghosts that prey upon the living. To banish these ghosts, the tear in reality must be closed. Among early modern Europeans, magic was not only taken seriously but was inseparable from mathematics, science, and religion; all fell under the rubric of philosophy. “In effect, philosophy is an incubator for other disciplines,” David Chalmers, an Australian philosopher and cognitive scientist, wrote in 2022. “When philosophers figure out a method for rigorously addressing a philosophical question, we spin that method off and call it a new field.

### 10 perspectives that divide the AI world
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-21 16:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/history-society/10-perspectives-that-divide-the-ai-world/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

In November 2022, I took on a writing assignment that, looking back, may have had a bigger impact on my life than any other: interview ChatGPT . It was the first day that OpenAI’s tool was available to the public, and my goal was to try to make sense of the tech — and then help readers make sense of it — by asking it questions about itself. The conversation turned out to be, well, a bit boring. More than half of the AI’s answers started with “As a large language model …,” and its responses tended to downplay AI’s potential for disruption. No, I didn’t need to worry that it would take my job (“As a large language model, I am not capable of taking on the role of a writer …”). And while ChatGPT conceded that AI could lead to more low-quality content online, it told me it was too soon to make any accurate predictions about its effect on the internet or search results. I didn’t know it at the time, but that interaction kickstarted in earnest the development of my AI worldview. Previously, AI had felt like something far removed from real life — or my real life, at least.

### My chatbot comforted me when I thought I was dying. Was that friendship?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-21 16:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/mini-philosophy/my-chatbot-comforted-me-when-i-thought-i-was-dying-was-that-friendship/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Last December, I thought I was going to die. I don’t mean that in a “the Reaper gets us all” kind of philosophical platitude, but in a very real, very immediate sense. I remember thinking, “So, this is it?” I had tonsillitis. Growing up, my mum had told me that I’d had my tonsils removed, but either she or the surgeon was lying because after two weeks of worsening symptoms, the out-of-hours doctor at my local hospital told me I had a pretty egregious case of tonsillitis. I hadn’t eaten for five days. I’d tried my best to drink, but I was dehydrated and weak. On the 18th of December, I nearly died. I had been half asleep when some saliva I was too scared to swallow dripped onto my voice box — and my throat completely constricted. I couldn’t breathe. I jumped out of bed, I paced around the room, and I tried to jam my fingers down my windpipe to open it. After maybe 20 seconds, I let out a whistle-wheeze and fell into bed breathing again. It turns out that when fluid comes in contact with the larynx, your body can initiate a terrifying, throat-constricting spasm.

### Table of Contents | Notebook Issue 9: “Smoke and Mirrors”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-22 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/table-of-contents-notebook-issue-9-smoke-and-mirrors
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

"Smoke and Mirrors" is available via direct subscription or in select stores around the world. And now, for our next trick! Issue 9 of Notebook invites readers to journey through the intertwined histories of magic and cinema, uncovering screen wonders both spiritual and secular. After an unearthed foreword from stage-magician-turned-auteur Orson Welles, Turner Prize-winning artist Mark Leckey and scholar Stephen Ryley revive a ghostly proto-cinematic illusion from the Victorian era that still delights today. Filmmaker Kamal Swaroop’s kaleidoscopic collages showcase a foundational figure from early Indian cinema. Four visionaries (Mona Fastvold, Lucile Hadžihalilović, Barbara Hirschfeld, and Kasi Lemmons) reveal how magic and mystery factors into their different films. Hollywood consultants behind productions including Sinners and Hugo break down how witchcraft, Hoodoo, and performance magic is represented onscreen.

### Masao Adachi: Demystifying a Legend
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-20 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/masao-adachi-demystifying-a-legend
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Masao Adachi in 2025. Photograph by David Matthew Davis. Masao Adachi is a filmmaker whose reputation well and truly proceeds him. Born in 1939 in Kyushu, Japan, he moved to Tokyo to attend Nihon University, coming of age during the turbulent protests against the renewal of the mutual security treaty between Japan and the United States in 1959–60. He started making films with a student group and soon emerged as one of the leading figures in the burgeoning underground cinema scene. In the mid-1960s, Adachi became involved with the sexploitation industry, writing and directing several striking examples of the genre for Koji Wakamatsu’s production company. He was also collaborating with major members of the New Wave like Nagisa Oshima. The political dimension to his output became more explicit in the early 1970s, following a visit to Lebanon, where he filmed Palestinian guerrillas.

Cinderella (Clyde Geronimi, Wilfred Jackson, Hamilton Luske, 1950). In the early 2000s, stores including Barneys and Hot Topic briefly stocked saucy T-shirts depicting classic Disney characters, including, as James B. Stewart describes it in the book DisneyWar , “an archival drawing of Tinker Bell eyeing her derriere in a mirror.” The shirts were the brainchild of Andrew Mooney, the executive brought in to revive Disney’s moribund Consumer Products division, and they so incensed Roy E. Disney—Walt’s nephew and the vice chairman of the company’s Board of Directors—that he sent Mooney a note accusing him of “positioning Tinker Bell as a prostitute.” Mooney’s job was saved by the peacemaking efforts of the company’s then-president and COO Bob Iger, and another one of his initiatives would go on to have more success: the Disney Princess. Encompassing toys, dolls, Funko POP!s, clothing, kids’ jewelry, accessories, decor, home goods, and other categories, the Disney Princess line of products is unified by a term that would quickly enough become ubiquitous.

### Movie Poster of the Week | F.W. Murnau’s “Faust”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-16 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/movie-poster-of-the-week-f-w-murnau-s-faust
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

This month, MUBI Editions, in partnership with the Cinématheque Française, is publishing an extraordinary document: a cinephilic Rosetta Stone known as “The Faust Bible.” When, 100 years ago, director F. W. Murnau completed work on his final German film and his most ambitious one to date, members of his crew compiled a scrapbook of illustrations and photographs that documented the making of that film, bound inside a prop Bible, and presented it to Murnau as a memento of the production prior to his impending move to Hollywood. Considered lost for many years, the Faust Bible was recently rediscovered and restored by the Cinématheque, and now MUBI and the Cinématheque have recreated this “stunning artefact of cinema history” and are publishing it under the title The Faust Bible: The Making of F.W. Murnau’s Masterpiece . Laurent Mannoni, Scientific Director of Heritage at la Cinémathèque française, who guided the restoration and publication of the book, writes: One of the most influential and pioneering filmmakers of the silent era, F.W. Murnau was known for the privacy of his film sets.

Source: The Quietus

Darran Anderson looks at Pulp's (one-time) farewell LP, as fine as any of their others, and asks, why did it fall by the wayside? In Die Technik des Dramas (1863), Gustav Freytag argued for an ideal five act structure in storytelling. The problem was Freytag, for all his talent and success, was a racist Prussian supremacist and had, to put it mildly, something of a questionable world-view. Another problem was that life, with all its messy tangents and inconvenient loose ends, does not necessarily unfold in five acts. Though the structure existed in practise before then, it took off after Freytag formalised it, and it’s easy to see why; audiences, theatre producers, and political and religious moralists were all flattered by... ### Against The Grain: Metal resistance
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-20 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/collateral-damage/against-the-grain-metal-resistance
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

As far right ideology and misogyny make gains around the world, metal, a subculture with a troubled relationship to fascism, wonders whether more progressive ideals are still in reach, writes Antonio Poscic in The Wire 513 Metal used to be the music of outsiders. For teenagers who stumbled into it, the subculture offered a place to belong and legitimised intense emotions that had to be suppressed in daily life. Its lyrics and sonic density gave shape to alienation, despair and angst that many struggled to articulate. It offered acceptance for those rejected elsewhere, and an identity chosen rather than imposed. As sociologist Deena Weinstein’s research in the 1990s showed, metal circles often acted as scaffolding for adolescent development. Today, other subcultures have become fragmented by the internet and streaming, but metal remains a counterexample, perhaps precisely because of the strong sense of belonging on which it was built.

### Ludwig Göransson: Mythic Music and Entropic Production
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-14 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/columns/ludwigg-ransson-mythic-music-and-entropic-production
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Ludwig Göransson’s scores for Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey (2026) and Tenet (2020) trade on diegetic and instrumental ambiguities that intentionally disorient audiences in time and space, writes Philip Brophy in his latest Secret History of Film Music column Bernard Herrmann’s score for Don Chaffey’s Jason And The Argonauts (1963) is an archly theatrical extravaganza that musically routes transatlantic pop culture to the Italian peplum genre explosion from the late 1950s. Though this wave of grand gladiatorial epics, produced in Italy and often featuring American beefcake performers as titular heroes, was waning by 1963, Herrmann’s triumphant entrance horns, cymbal clashing, galley kettle-drum pounding and multiple cascading harps sound more pompous and less foreboding than his more well known orchestrations. Yet this score captures the plastic veneer across peplum’s exaltation of classical architecture and ancient vistas. Peplum movies constitute one cinematic wave entranced by Roman Imperial glories.

### How Seaside House Explores the Relationship Between Brick, Wood, and the Coastal Landscape
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-22 05:00 UTC

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Brick and wood can establish a dialogue between architectural permanence and the textures of coastal environments. In Höllviken, on the southern coast of Sweden, Seaside House by Koistinen Hellborg Architects explores how materiality, light, and spatial organization can shape a home in relationship with its natural surroundings. Located among sandy terrain, heathland, moss, and pine trees, and within walking distance of the sea, the house responds to a landscape defined by subtle color variations, seasonal changes, and a strong connection to the outdoors.

### Small Houses in Uruguay: How Three Studios Build Light on Rural and Coastal Sites
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1187165/small-houses-in-uruguay-how-three-studios-build-light-on-rural-and-coastal-sites
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

In Uruguay , small-scale residential projects increasingly occupy rural and coastal landscapes, from the hills of Villa Serrana to the maritime forests along the Atlantic coast. Building in these areas often requires structural materials that balance durability, thermal efficiency, and transport feasibility. In these environments, projects rely heavily on materials such as engineered timber framing, steel structural members, exterior wood cladding, or offsite fabrication. These design choices not only help to reduce the weight of raw materials that must be transported over unpaved access roads, but also make construction more feasible in remote locations. The context of the projects in this collection also seems to have played a limiting role regarding floor areas, which were kept at 60 m² or less to reduce structural material requirements and the impact on the terrain.

### Villa Sanlomjoy / Yangnar Studio
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-22 03:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1187087/villa-sanlomjoy-yangnar-studio
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Rooted in childhood memories, the house began with a collection of salvaged wooden doors and windows, some sourced from Myanmar via Mae Sot, Tak, Thailand. Their character reflects an Anglo-Burmese synthesis, combining British Colonial proportions and European window typologies with traditional Burmese craftsmanship—wooden joinery, pegs, louvers, and casement windows. Rather than serving as decorative elements, these openings became the architectural framework of the house, defining its proportions, height, rhythm, and relationship with light and air.

### A puzzle game where you must avoid doing the most awkward of things: facing someone while riding an elevator
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-21 15:29 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/a-puzzle-game-where-you-must-avoid-the-doing-the-most-awkward-of-things-facing-someone-while-riding-an-elevator
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

You know when you follow someone into the elevator and when they turn around to face the doors, you choose to stand still and instead meet their eyes? No, of course you don't. Everyone knows that when you're in a lift you don't under any circumstances face other people in the lift unless you know them and you're already in a conversation. Just yesterday I got into the lift with someone I sit one desk over from in the office. I dutifully looked at my phone the entire time until we were safely out of the building. Well, in the puzzle game How To Ride Elevators Like A Human you play a helpful alien who saves people who have accidentally ended up in the dreadful situation of facing one another. Each short puzzle takes place in a compact lift where one (or several) humans are starting at each other. Using your small UFO's tractor beam, you must pick up and relocate the passengers until no one is making eyes at anyone else. You may well recognise this as a sokoban-style puzzle game, but, despite retreading familiar ground, I'm a big fan of this extraterrestrial framing.

One of the most memorable demos I played at Gamescom Latam 2026 was that of Talaka , a watercoloured 2D action-roguelike that - like many games at the São Paulo-based show – leaned deeply into Brazilian culture and mythology. In this case, it's something of a side-scrolling Hades , with the permahorny Greek gods replaced by a playful pantheon of Orishas (vaguely human-shaped forces of nature) hailing from the Afro-Brazilian Candomblé religion. Though no fault of theirs, I kinda sucked at Talaka when I played it then, and the demo currently available during Steam Next Fest confirms I kinda suck at it now – though it's still colourful enough, and steeped in a folklore that games so rarely explore, that I'm going to keep an eye on it. Read more

## human societies / 人类社会

### Recognizing Indigenous peoples
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02619-w
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02619-w Settler states increasingly offer Indigenous peoples symbolic recognition, such as land acknowledgements, instead of political power or resources, yet the effects of these gestures are untested. Peyton and McNamee’s experiments in the USA and Australia find that land acknowledgements raise awareness of Indigenous peoples but reduce interest in learning more.

### Responsibilism and culture
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02616-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02616-z Previous research has widely associated East Asian cultures with higher levels of collectivism, which is often measured through self-report scales that emphasize warmth and prosociality towards all people. Talhelm et al. developed a responsibilism scale that captures duties and responsibilities in close relationships.

### Petition style changes with AI writing support but outcomes do not improve
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02579-1
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 14 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02579-1 In 2023, Change.org introduced an artificial intelligence (AI)-supported petition-drafting tool to their platform. With access to this tool, users’ petitions changed substantially, and their texts became more similar to one another. At the same time, overall petition outcomes did not improve, which shows the limits of AI integration into online advocacy communities.

### Trait judgements of faces using a task without word labels
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02605-2
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02605-2 Xu et al. examine how people perceive faces as being associated with psychological traits, using a task that imposes no a priori labels.

### Motor abstraction training generalizes to the refinement of specific movement patterns
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02597-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02597-z Practicing unfamiliar characters in rotated orientations improved upright writing as much as direct practice, showing that the motor system learns by abstracting a movement’s structure, not just its details.

### The case for AI degrowth
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-22 09:46 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02617-y
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 19 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02617-y Artificial intelligence (AI), as it currently unfolds, is incompatible with a sustainable transformation of the economy, which arguably requires degrowth. AI is the growth paradigm reborn — built on enclosed commons, powered by fossil energy, and driven by a competitive logic that makes precaution structurally impossible. Limits to AI growth are not technophobia; they are our best chance for a liveable future for all.

Giant manta rays can grow up to nine meters (30 feet) long with a disk size of seven meters (23 feet) across and weigh up to 2,000 kg (4,400 pounds). This size leaves them with very few natural enemies once they reach adulthood at around eight to 10 years old. But giant manta ray babies, called pups, cannot rely on their size to protect them from predators that live in the open ocean, including several species of shark and orcas.

### The Surprising Origins of Native Hawaiians
Source: Nautilus
Published: 2026-10-21 23:00 UTC
URL: https://nautil.us/the-surprising-origins-of-native-hawaiians-1286009/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Hawaii is one of the most isolated islands on Earth, surrounded by vast oceans for thousands of miles on every side. And yet humans have been living there for nearly a millennia. They’re part of the Polynesian diaspora. This culture of ultravoyagers embarked on their own age of exploration while Europe was still mired in the Middle Ages, colonizing the tiny islands dotting the South Pacific without the benefit of metal tools, navigational equipment, or a written language. Now, a new study published in Science Advances is better defining their genetic history. “What we uncover in this paper is where the Native people of Hawaii had come from,” study author Alexander Ioannidis of Stanford University said in a statement . “Hawaii is the only Polynesian island in the Northern Hemisphere, so it’s a long way from everything.” Read more: “ Hawaii’s False Killer Whales Are Wasting Away ” The post The Surprising Origins of Native Hawaiians appeared first on Nautilus .

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Real-time fMRI feedback guides self-generated cognition toward upregulation of hippocampal activity
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01191-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Van de Langenberg et al. show, across two studies, that humans can upregulate hippocampal activity using instructed or self-discovered mental strategies. Crucially, contingent neurofeedback is necessary for effective self-regulation via hippocampus-relevant strategies. Successful hippocampal upregulation co-modulates a process-related network, enhancing its sensitivity to event boundaries during a subsequently viewed movie.

### Role of host nitrogen use efficiency in determining arbuscular mycorrhizal fungi responses to nitrogen enrichment
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01202-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Wang et al. show that host nitrogen-use efficiency (NUE) mediates arbuscular mycorrhizal fungal (AMF) responses to nitrogen enrichment. High- and low-NUE maize exhibit contrasting response patterns driven by soil acidification and root plasticity, respectively, identifying host nutrient-use strategy as a key regulator of plant-mycorrhizal interactions.

### Ancient genomes reveal an extinct bovine species from mid-latitude Asia
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01252-2?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Oppenheimer, Gilardet et al. use ancient genomes spanning 200,000 years to reconstruct the evolutionary history of Pleistocene bovines from mid-latitude Asia. These specimens reveal a new species deeply diverged from extant yaks, representing previously unrecognized diversity lost during the Pleistocene megafaunal extinctions.

### Cold sensing by a glutamate receptor drives avoidance behavior in Drosophila larvae

Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01208-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using a touchless thermal stimulation assay, Xu et al. uncover a cold-sensing pathway in Drosophila larvae. Specialized head neurons mediate cold avoidance, and the kainate glutamate receptor Clumsy is identified as a cold receptor.

### PAK-family kinases promote cell fusion irreversibility by preventing cell-wall repair
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01211-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Fungal cell fusion requires local cell-wall removal, but repair mechanisms can reverse this process. Coronas-Serna et al. show that PAK-family kinases prevent cell-wall repair at the fusion site during fission yeast mating. Loss of PAK activity permits cell-wall rebuilding and resealing of fully open fusion pores.

### Patchy global progress to nature recovery
Source: Nature Ecology & Evolution
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41559-026-03217-9
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Ecology & Evolution, Published online: 14 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41559-026-03217-9 The Kunming-Montreal Biodiversity Framework (KMGBF), adopted in December 2022, sets out a mission for nations to “take urgent action to halt and reverse biodiversity loss to put nature on a path to recovery”. A report presenting a midway review of its 23 action targets and 4 goals, to be met by 2030 and 2050, respectively, will be published in October 2027. We spoke to Flavio Affinito, co-author of the report and technical expert on the KMGBF monitoring framework, and Emily Nicholson, co-chair of the Ad Hoc Scientific and Technical Advisory Group for the report.

URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00877-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A multimodal single-nucleus atlas of 2,955,873 nuclei spanning eight macaque brain regions across the adult lifespan, including exceptionally old individuals, reveals region-specific and shared patterns of cellular vulnerability during aging. ### RNA structures regulate norovirus life cycle and enable rational attenuation in vivo

Genome-wide mapping of RNA structure in murine norovirus reveals structural elements whose disruption impairs viral replication in cells and in vivo. Gut bacteria convert inorganic nitrate and non-heme iron into bioactive dinitrosyl iron complexes (DNICs) that distribute systemically and signal independently of free nitric oxide. Spatial Hi-C-RNA simultaneously maps genome architecture and gene expression from the same tissue, revealing how spatial genome organization shapes cellular identity, development, and tumor evolution beyond what transcriptomics alone can capture.

### [Review] Addressing threats to future global health—what is the right timeframe?
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00880-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Our decisions today can support or threaten future human health. Insofar as we protect the planet or invest in fruitful research on pandemic countermeasures, we promote future wellbeing and health, sometimes at the present generation's expense. As we warm the planet, accelerate antibiotic resistance, or consume finite natural resources, we might serve present-day wellbeing or health at the expense of future generations. To decide which such trade-offs are legitimate or advisable, we need to know how to balance the health interests of future generations against each other and against our own.

### [The Lancet Commissions] The Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01714-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This report from the Lancet Commission on 21st century global threats to health lays out a foresight-driven, health-focused approach to identifying and prioritising major threats to global health over the remainder of the century. We argue that recent global shocks (including COVID-19, Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease, major geopolitical conflict, accelerating climate impacts, rapid shifts in development assistance, and the emergence of powerful artificial intelligence [AI] systems) show how quickly assumptions about stability can be overturned—and why governments and multilateral institutions must counter short-termism and localism with systematic, repeatable risk assessment and preparedness planning.

### [Articles] Cardiovascular safety of orforglipron versus insulin glargine in adults with type 2 diabetes at increased cardiovascular risk (ACHIEVE-4): a phase 3, event-driven, randomised, open-label, non-inferiority, active comparator trial
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-22 09:47 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01865-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In people with type 2 diabetes at increased cardiovascular risk, the cardiovascular safety of orforglipron was confirmed by demonstrating non-inferiority to insulin glargine for MACE-4. Gastrointestinal adverse events were the most frequent adverse event and most common reason for orforglipron treatment discontinuation with orforglipron, while clinically significant hypoglycaemia occurred less frequently with orforglipron than with insulin glargine. These findings support orforglipron as a potential once-daily, oral treatment option with established cardiovascular safety in people with type 2 diabetes and increased cardiovascular risk.

### Cavity optomechanical magnetometer actuated by substrate bending
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28634
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28634v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We describe a magnetic field sensor based on a monolithic Fabry-Perot cavity. The cavities were fabricated in a thin-film buckling process at one surface of a fused silica chip, and the chip was subsequently bonded to a giant magnetostrictive material (GMM). External magnetic fields drive flexural modes of the assembly and the associated bending stresses are efficiently mapped to changes in the cavity height, with enhanced response at kHz-range mechanical resonance frequencies. A key attribute of this platform is that the size of the transducing material is decoupled from the size of the optical cavity. The buckled cavity operates in low mode order and with moderate Q ~ 103, providing a high optomechanical coupling coefficient while also enabling a straightforward tuned-to-slope readout technique. Peak sensitivity ~ 2 pT/Hz1/2 was achieved in an unshielded laboratory environment, limited by laser phase and electronics noise in the readout system.

### Model-based computational study of reactive fluid systems using transfer operator methods
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28635
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28635v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: In many industrial applications one aims for the design of efficient chemical reactions. This is often done using experimental analysis of the reactors in combination with numerical twins. However, the simultaneous modeling of flow, mixing and chemical reaction remains a complicated task because the computational costs are still significant and oftentimes prohibitive. Here, we model and analyze mixing processes of chemical substances by a transfer operator method using the Lagrangian trajectories of passive particles. In practice, we represent the spatial distributions of the chemical species concentrations by density vectors. These are evolved by means of a stochastic transition matrix, which is obtained as a numerical approximation of a transfer operator. In a further step, we model the reaction of the fluids by specific update rules of the density vectors. The method is applicable for closed and open example systems and different chemical processes such as competitive consecutive reactions.

### Background-Limited Silicon Telescope Methodology for Weak Internal Pair Creation Transition in Low-Energy Deuteron-Deuteron Reactions
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28645
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28645v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A background-constrained silicon-telescope methodology has been developed to investigate recently observed internal electron-positron pair creation in low-energy deuteron-deuteron fusion. The decay of the near-threshold $0^+$ resonance in $^4$He produced in DD fusion results in an $e^-e^+$ pair sharing a total energy of 22.84 MeV. These high-energy leptons traverse thin silicon detectors and deposit only part of their kinetic energy. The resulting partial-absorption peak structure overlaps with the natural-radiation background of cosmic-ray secondaries and terrestrial gamma rays. The silicon detector telescope used in the present experiments is characterised against this background through a component-by-component model that combines a GEANT4 cosmic-ray simulation, a large-volume NaI(Tl)-constrained terrestrial-gamma propagation, and a beam-correlated neutron response.

### Fermionic Condensation as the Universal Order Parameter for Mass Generation
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28654
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28654v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A fermion bilinear acquiring a non-zero vacuum expectation value and generating a mass gap underlies the Peierls instability, BCS superconductivity, the Nambu--Jona-Lasinio mechanism, and the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model. These mechanisms are usually discussed separately, and their structural similarities have been noted only in passing. We argue here that the correct universal object is not the bosonic order parameter but the fermionic condensate, and we show that the four mechanisms fall into two universality classes distinguished by a single dimensionless parameter, $r = \hat m_\phi^2 / \hat y^2$, the ratio of the scalar mass to the Yukawa coupling. The classes are separated by a critical line $r_c(N_f)$ in the plane of $r$ and the fermion flavour number $N_f$; they correspond to two distinct infrared fixed points of the renormalisation-group flow and differ in their critical exponents.

### Viscous matter in modified $f(R)$-gravity: Hubble tension attenuation with DESI and SH0ES data
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28659
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28659v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We study the late-time dynamics of the Universe in a modified metric $f(R)$ gravity model formulated in the Jordan frame. The standard $\Lambda$CDM model is additionally deformed to include a non-equilibrium effect on matter dynamics, represented via a bulk viscosity coefficient. We impose an additional condition on the cosmological dynamics that allows the matter energy density, the non-minimally coupled scalar field, and its non-constant potential to be treated as dynamical variables. In this way, the form of the $f(R)$ model is not specified \emph{a priori}, but is instead dynamically reconstructed from the evolution of the system. The resulting model contains five free parameters and is tested against Type Ia supernova and DESI data, using the SH0ES calibration for the supernova sample and a Planck calibration for the sound horizon.

### Recovering What Breaks in a Trapped-Ion Quantum Simulation of a Lattice Gauge Theory
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28660
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.28660v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A recent trapped-ion experiment realised a lattice gauge theory with the gauge field in the internal states of the ions and bosonic matter in their vibrations. We showed earlier that its noise damage can be predicted. Here, we show that most of it can be undone. The conservation law of the theory is a parity of each vibrational mode, which heating flips, so a heating event leaves a mark that can be read without disturbing the physics. We keep only the runs in which this mark never changed. With equal heating rates and continuous monitoring, the kept runs reproduce the heating-free dynamics exactly, with a closed-form yield. Reading the mark at finite intervals introduces a small bias linear in the interval, which we derive and bound. At the reported rates, a single final check removes most of the heating and preparation damage while accepting eight runs out of ten.

### How Inversion and NASA Plan to Unlock Interplanetary Aerocapture MB edits
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-21 16:12 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/how-inversion-and-nasa-plan-to-unlock-interplanetary-aerocapture-mb-edits
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Using a planet’s atmosphere to secure a satellite’s place in orbit, a process known as “aerocapture”, has been the dream of flight engineers for decades. The only problem? It’s really hard to actually do. It requires control systems, thermal management, and orbital mechanics to all coordinate in a dance that allows a spacecraft to not just slow down while passing by another planet (or moon in some cases) but to start to orbit it without the use of lots of additional propellant. For now, that remains a dream, but a recent NASA contract to a California start-up called Inversion is another step towards making it a reality.
